[codex] ## Operating Rules
[codex] 
[codex] - Do not use agent or subagent tools for code exploration. Inspect the workspace
[codex]   directly with `rg`, `find`, and file reads.
[codex] - Never run Git write commands, including `commit`, `push`, `checkout`, `reset`,
[codex]   or branch manipulation, unless the user explicitly requests them. The user
[codex]   controls Git operations.
[codex] - Keep implementations simple. Choose the most direct design that handles
[codex]   realistic inputs, states, and failure modes without weakening security, data
[codex]   integrity, or required behavior.
[codex] - Put request shape and syntax validation at the API boundary. Put business
[codex]   invariants, ownership, persistence state, and cross-entity rules in services.
[codex] - Do not duplicate validation across layers unless another caller can bypass the
[codex]   validated boundary or the service owns the rule.
[codex] - Add defensive branches only for states that can plausibly arise and have a
[codex]   useful response. Handle failures at external and asynchronous boundaries
[codex]   explicitly.
[codex] - Never commit credentials or replace missing infrastructure, credentials, or
[codex]   shared artifacts with hard-coded workarounds.
[codex]

[codex] ## Architecture and Security Rules
[codex] 
[codex] - Keep controllers thin and delegate application behavior to services.
[codex]   Controllers must never import repositories.
[codex] - Keep consumers thin and delegate to services. Consumers must never import
[codex]   repositories.
[codex] - Keep transaction boundaries in the service layer and persistence behind
[codex]   repositories.
[codex] - Use Jakarta Persistence APIs only. Never import `org.hibernate.*` or depend on
[codex]   Hibernate-specific behavior.
[codex] - Extend the shared auditable or soft-deletable base entity that matches the
[codex]   domain lifecycle; consult the shared Spring Boot conventions before creating
[codex]   an entity.
[codex] - Access external exchange-rate systems through `ExchangeRateProvider`. The
[codex]   service layer must never reference FRED-specific clients or types.
[codex] - Publish domain events from services. Do not inject message publishers into
[codex]   services; bridge domain events to external messages in listeners.
[codex] - Apply `@SchedulerLock` to scheduled work that must execute once across pods.
[codex] - Cache read paths only where the documented cache strategy calls for it, and
[codex]   evict affected cache entries after imports or mutations.
[codex] - Preserve claims-header-based authorization and enforce endpoint permissions
[codex]   with `@PreAuthorize`. Do not trust client-supplied identity or permission
[codex]   headers outside the platform's validated gateway path.
[codex] - Treat role definitions and atomic permission ownership as permission-service
[codex]   concerns. Do not redefine the ecosystem RBAC model in this repository.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Java and Test Standards
[codex] 
[codex] Before writing or modifying Java, read and apply
[codex] [code-quality-standards.md](../service-common/docs/code-quality-standards.md).
[codex] Do not skip this step. In particular, use `var` where the standard requires it,
[codex] use descriptive names, avoid wildcard imports, and end the first sentence of
[codex] Javadoc with a period.

[codex] Before writing or changing tests, read the relevant section of
[codex] [testing-patterns.md](../service-common/docs/testing-patterns.md):
[codex]

[codex] - Read the integration and Testcontainers guidance for persistence, messaging,
[codex]   cache, or application-context tests.
[codex] - Test correct behavior and edge cases. Fix defects instead of testing around
[codex]   them.
[codex] - Do not weaken, delete, or disable existing tests to make a change pass.
[codex] - Keep reusable test values in the repository's test fixtures or constants
[codex]   instead of scattering magic values.

[codex] # Service-Common Artifact Resolution
[codex] 
[codex] This document explains the split between local `mavenLocal()` resolution and
[codex] the GitHub Packages path used by GitHub Actions and release builds.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Version Scope
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` is a shared Java library version, not the deployment version
[codex] for the whole Budget Analyzer stack. Bump and release it when
[codex] `spring-platform`, `spring-cloud-platform`, `service-core`, or `service-web`
[codex] changes. Do not change it only because a service image, frontend artifact,
[codex] route, production overlay, `/api-docs` metadata file, or OCI deployment
[codex] configuration changed.
[codex] 
[codex] Java services can release runtime artifacts while continuing to consume the
[codex] same checked-in `serviceCommon` coordinate from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`.
[codex] The production deployment identity is the digest-pinned image inventory and
[codex] deployment manifest, which may contain mixed service artifact versions.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Local Contributor Flow
[codex] 
[codex] The supported side-by-side workspace flow must stay credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] If you follow [getting-started.md](getting-started.md) and run local
[codex] development through orchestration and Tilt:
[codex] 
[codex] - clone the repos side by side
[codex] - open the sibling `orchestration/` repo
[codex] - run `./setup.sh`
[codex] - run `tilt up`
[codex] 
[codex] you should not need `GITHUB_ACTOR`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, or a personal access token
[codex] just to start the app locally.
[codex] 
[codex] That works because the local build path stays local-first:
[codex] 
[codex] - Java service repos keep `mavenLocal()` first
[codex] - Tilt runs the `service-common-publish` resource first
[codex] - `service-common-publish` runs `publishToMavenLocal`
[codex] - downstream host-side Gradle builds then resolve `service-common` from Maven
[codex]   Local
[codex] 
[codex] If you hit a local `service-common` resolution error while working in the full
[codex] workspace, republish it locally and rerun the service build:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../service-common
[codex] ./gradlew clean build publishToMavenLocal
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## When GitHub Packages Credentials Are Required
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub Packages credentials are a CI/release concern, not a getting-started
[codex] prerequisite.
[codex] 
[codex] You need GitHub Packages credentials when all of these are true:
[codex] 
[codex] - the build is not using the local orchestration/Tilt path
[codex] - `service-common` is not already available through Maven Local
[codex] - the build must resolve the published `org.budgetanalyzer` artifacts remotely
[codex]   from `https://maven.pkg.github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common`
[codex] 
[codex] Typical examples:
[codex] 
[codex] - default GitHub Actions `build.yml` runs in `transaction-service`,
[codex]   `currency-service`, `permission-service`, and `session-gateway`
[codex] - release-image Docker builds
[codex] - isolated CI or clean-builder image builds
[codex] - manual clean-shell verification of remote published artifacts
[codex] 
[codex] `service-common` itself now refreshes the current checked-in snapshot from
[codex] `main` through its `publish-snapshot.yml` workflow. That remote snapshot path
[codex] exists to support isolated CI/release builds; it does not change the local
[codex] contributor contract.
[codex] 
[codex] For the Java consumer repos, the default build workflow contract is now:
[codex] 
[codex] - read the pinned `serviceCommon` version from `gradle/libs.versions.toml`
[codex] - fail fast if `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME` or
[codex]   `SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN` is missing
[codex] - preflight the versioned `service-core` and `service-web` POM URLs in GitHub
[codex]   Packages
[codex] - run `./gradlew build` with `GITHUB_ACTOR` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` exported from
[codex]   that same secret pair
[codex] 
[codex] ## GitHub Actions Environment
[codex] 
[codex] When a workflow intentionally resolves `service-common` remotely from GitHub
[codex] Packages, Gradle needs both a token and a package username.
[codex] 
[codex] For cross-repo private Maven/Gradle consumption, do not assume the workflow
[codex] repo's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can read `service-common`. GitHub still treats these
[codex] packages as repository-scoped, so the consuming workflow needs an explicit
[codex] GitHub Packages credential.
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the current repo wiring expects:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] `GITHUB_ACTOR` here means the username that owns the GitHub Packages credential,
[codex] not necessarily `${{ github.actor }}`. `GITHUB_TOKEN` here means the secret used
[codex] for remote package reads, not necessarily the workflow repo's default token.
[codex] This requirement applies to the remote GitHub Packages path used by default
[codex] `build.yml` and release workflows. It does not apply to the normal `tilt up`
[codex] contributor flow.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Containerized Release Builds
[codex] 
[codex] The Java service Dockerfiles now support remote `service-common` resolution for
[codex] release and isolated CI builds without copying host `.m2` state into the build
[codex] context and without checking out sibling source trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Pass the workflow credentials into BuildKit as secrets:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] docker build \
[codex]   --secret id=github_actor,env=GITHUB_ACTOR \
[codex]   --secret id=github_token,env=GITHUB_TOKEN \
[codex]   -f Dockerfile .
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In GitHub Actions, the release job should still expose those same credential
[codex] values under the env names the current Gradle builds read:
[codex] 
[codex] ```yaml
[codex] env:
[codex]   GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]   GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The Dockerfiles read those values only inside the Gradle builder-stage `RUN`
[codex] steps, so the credentials are not written into the image, filesystem layers, or
[codex] checked-in repo files.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Why Public Package Visibility Does Not Remove This
[codex] 
[codex] Making the `service-common` Maven packages public in the GitHub UI does not
[codex] remove the Maven/Gradle authentication requirement.
[codex] 
[codex] Keep the release workflow package-visibility and workflow-access cleanup, but do not
[codex] treat it as the fix for local contributor UX. The fix for local contributor UX
[codex] is keeping the side-by-side workspace and Tilt flow local-first and
[codex] credential-free.
[codex] 
[codex] exec
[codex] /bin/bash -lc "find . -maxdepth 3 -type f -not -path './.git/*' | sort; sed -n '1,280p' build.gradle.kts; sed -n '1,240p' settings.gradle.kts; sed -n '1,260p' gradle/libs.versions.toml; for file in .github/workflows/*; do echo \"FILE "'$file"; sed -n '"'1,300p' \""'$file"; done; sed -n '"'1,260p' README.md; sed -n '1,280p' docs/local-development.md" in /workspace/currency-service
[codex]  succeeded in 0ms:

[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     branches: [main]
[codex]   pull_request:
[codex]     branches: [main]
[codex]   workflow_dispatch:
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: read
[codex] 
[codex] env:
[codex]   FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex]

[codex]   build:
[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] 
[codex]     steps:
[codex]       - name: Checkout code
[codex]         uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up JDK 25
[codex]         uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex]         with:
[codex]           java-version: '25'
[codex]           distribution: 'temurin'
[codex]

[codex]         env:
[codex]           GITHUB_ACTOR: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]           GITHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex]         run: ./gradlew build
[codex]

[codex]         if: always()
[codex]         uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
[codex]         with:

[codex]           retention-days: 7

[codex] FILE .github/workflows/publish-release.yml
[codex] name: Publish Release
[codex] 
[codex] on:
[codex]   push:
[codex]     tags:
[codex]       - "v*"
[codex]   workflow_dispatch:
[codex]     inputs:
[codex]       release_ref:
[codex]         description: "Existing Git ref to build; SemVer tags publish X.Y.Z by default"
[codex]         required: true
[codex]         type: string
[codex]       docker_label:
[codex]         description: "Optional Docker tag label for an ad hoc manual image; never use latest"
[codex]         required: false
[codex]         type: string
[codex] 
[codex] permissions:
[codex]   contents: read
[codex] 
[codex] env:
[codex]   FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] 
[codex] jobs:
[codex]   publish:
[codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex]     permissions:
[codex]       contents: read
[codex]       packages: write
[codex]     env:
[codex]       IMAGE_NAME: ghcr.io/budgetanalyzer/currency-service
[codex] 
[codex]     steps:
[codex]       - name: Checkout code
[codex]         uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex]         with:
[codex]           ref: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && github.event.inputs.release_ref || github.ref_name }}
[codex]           fetch-depth: 0
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Resolve release inputs
[codex]         id: release
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         env:
[codex]           REQUESTED_RELEASE_REF: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && github.event.inputs.release_ref || github.ref_name }}
[codex]           REQUESTED_DOCKER_LABEL: ${{ github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && github.event.inputs.docker_label || '' }}
[codex]           SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]           SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           event_name="${GITHUB_EVENT_NAME}"
[codex]           release_ref="${REQUESTED_RELEASE_REF}"
[codex]           requested_docker_label="${REQUESTED_DOCKER_LABEL}"
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${release_ref}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "release_ref is required." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${release_ref}" =~ ^v[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]]; then
[codex]             default_docker_label="${release_ref#v}"
[codex]           else
[codex]             default_docker_label=""
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${event_name}" == "push" ]]; then
[codex]             if [[ -z "${default_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]               echo "Release tag pushes must use SemVer tags like v1.2.3, got ${release_ref}." >&2
[codex]               exit 1
[codex]             fi
[codex]             if [[ -n "${requested_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]               echo "docker_label is only supported for workflow_dispatch." >&2
[codex]               exit 1
[codex]             fi
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ "${event_name}" == "workflow_dispatch" && -z "${default_docker_label}" && -z "${requested_docker_label}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "workflow_dispatch release_ref is not a SemVer release tag, so docker_label is required." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           docker_label="${requested_docker_label:-${default_docker_label}}"
[codex]           if [[ ! "${docker_label}" =~ ^[A-Za-z0-9_][A-Za-z0-9_.-]{0,127}$ || "${docker_label}" == "latest" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Resolved docker_label is not Docker-safe or is disallowed: ${docker_label}." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           if [[ -z "${SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME}" || -z "${SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN}" ]]; then
[codex]             echo "Missing SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME or SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN." >&2
[codex]             exit 1
[codex]           fi
[codex] 
[codex]           echo "docker_label=${docker_label}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Resolve checked-out revision
[codex]         id: source
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           set -euo pipefail
[codex] 
[codex]           commit_sha="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"
[codex]           echo "commit_sha=${commit_sha}" >> "${GITHUB_OUTPUT}"
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up QEMU
[codex]         uses: docker/setup-qemu-action@v4
[codex]         with:
[codex]           platforms: arm64
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Set up Docker Buildx
[codex]         uses: docker/setup-buildx-action@v4
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Log in to GHCR
[codex]         uses: docker/login-action@v4
[codex]         with:
[codex]           registry: ghcr.io
[codex]           username: ${{ github.actor }}
[codex]           password: ${{ secrets.GITHUB_TOKEN }}
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Build and push release image
[codex]         id: build
[codex]         uses: docker/build-push-action@v7
[codex]         with:
[codex]           context: .
[codex]           file: ./Dockerfile
[codex]           platforms: linux/arm64
[codex]           push: true
[codex]           tags: |
[codex]             ${{ env.IMAGE_NAME }}:${{ steps.release.outputs.docker_label }}
[codex]           labels: |
[codex]             org.opencontainers.image.source=${{ github.server_url }}/${{ github.repository }}
[codex]             org.opencontainers.image.revision=${{ steps.source.outputs.commit_sha }}
[codex]             org.opencontainers.image.version=${{ steps.release.outputs.docker_label }}
[codex]           secrets: |
[codex]             github_actor=${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_USERNAME }}
[codex]             github_token=${{ secrets.SERVICE_COMMON_PACKAGES_READ_TOKEN }}
[codex] 
[codex]       - name: Print digest-pinned reference
[codex]         shell: bash
[codex]         run: |
[codex]           echo "${IMAGE_NAME}:${{ steps.release.outputs.docker_label }}@${{ steps.build.outputs.digest }}"
[codex] # Currency Service
[codex] 
[codex] > "Archetype: service. Role: Manages currencies and exchange rates with external provider integration."
[codex] >
[codex] > — [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md#tree-position)
[codex] 
[codex] [![Build](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/currency-service/actions/workflows/build.yml)
[codex] 
[codex] Spring Boot microservice that automates exchange rate imports from the [Federal Reserve Economic Data (FRED)](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/) API, delivering reliable currency and exchange rate data for Budget Analyzer.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Core Capabilities
[codex] 
[codex] - **Automated FRED imports** - Daily scheduled imports with provider abstraction ([details](docs/fred-integration.md))
[codex] - **Currency series management** - CRUD operations for exchange rate time series ([API docs](docs/api/README.md))
[codex] - **Historical rate queries** - Date range filtering with Redis caching (80-95% hit rate)
[codex] - **Distributed coordination** - Multi-pod safe scheduling with ShedLock
[codex] - **Event-driven architecture** - Guaranteed delivery via transactional outbox + RabbitMQ
[codex] 
[codex] ## Quick Start
[codex] 
[codex] **Prerequisites:** JDK 25, Docker/Docker Compose
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # 1. Start infrastructure
[codex] cd ../orchestration && tilt up
[codex] 
[codex] # 2. Export credentials and start the service
[codex] cd ../currency-service
[codex] export FRED_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
[codex] export SPRING_DATASOURCE_PASSWORD=your_db_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PASSWORD=your_rabbitmq_password
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PASSWORD=your_redis_password
[codex] # See docs/local-development.md for full env var list (TLS, ports, etc.)
[codex] ./gradlew bootRun
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] **Dev server:** `http://localhost:8084` | **Swagger UI:** `http://localhost:8084/swagger-ui.html`
[codex] 
[codex] ## Technology Stack
[codex] 
[codex] Java 25, Spring Boot 3.x (Web, Data JPA, Modulith, Actuator), PostgreSQL, Redis, RabbitMQ, ShedLock, Flyway, SpringDoc OpenAPI, TestContainers + JUnit 5
[codex] 
[codex] ## Build and Test
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew clean build
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] JaCoCo coverage reports are written to
[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html` and
[codex] `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`. `check` enforces the
[codex] configured coverage gates.
[codex]

[codex] # Local Development
[codex] 
[codex] ## Prerequisites
[codex] 
[codex] - JDK 25
[codex] - Docker and Docker Compose (for infrastructure)
[codex] 
[codex] **Ecosystem setup guides:**
[codex] - [getting-started.md](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/getting-started.md)
[codex] - [database-setup.md](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/database-setup.md)
[codex] - [service-common artifact resolution](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/development/service-common-artifact-resolution.md)
[codex] 
[codex] > **Note:** This service uses the `currency` database (not `budget_analyzer`). Local builds resolve `service-common` from `mavenLocal()` — no GitHub credentials required.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Running Locally
[codex] 
[codex] Start shared infrastructure from the orchestration repo:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../orchestration
[codex] tilt up
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] In another terminal, export the required environment variables and start the service:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] cd ../currency-service
[codex] 
[codex] export FRED_API_KEY=your_api_key_here
[codex] export SPRING_DATASOURCE_PASSWORD=your_currency_database_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PASSWORD=your_currency_service_rabbitmq_password
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_HOST=localhost
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_PORT=5671
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_SSL_ENABLED=true
[codex] export SPRING_RABBITMQ_SSL_BUNDLE=infra-ca
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PASSWORD=your_currency_service_redis_password
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST="${SPRING_DATA_REDIS_HOST:-localhost}"
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PORT="${SPRING_DATA_REDIS_PORT:-6379}"
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_SSL_ENABLED=true
[codex] export SPRING_DATA_REDIS_SSL_BUNDLE=infra-ca
[codex] export INFRA_CA_CERT_PATH="file:$(cd ../orchestration && pwd)/nginx/certs/infra/infra-ca.pem"
[codex] 
[codex] ./gradlew bootRun
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] If you are reusing values from `../orchestration/.env`, map
[codex] `POSTGRES_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD`,
[codex] `RABBITMQ_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD`, and
[codex] `REDIS_CURRENCY_SERVICE_PASSWORD` into the Spring environment variables above.
[codex] 
[codex] ### TLS Notes
[codex] 
[codex] - The CA path must point at the host-side file created by `../orchestration/scripts/bootstrap/setup-infra-tls.sh`.
[codex] - RabbitMQ hostname verification is enabled. `SPRING_RABBITMQ_HOST` must use a name covered by the broker certificate SANs.
[codex] - The default host-side certificate includes `localhost`, `rabbitmq.infrastructure`, `rabbitmq.infrastructure.svc`, and `rabbitmq.infrastructure.svc.cluster.local`. Using a different hostname will fail the TLS handshake until the certificate is regenerated with that SAN.
[codex]

[codex] ## Building
[codex] 
[codex] Dependency versions for shared Spring libraries are managed by the service-common
[codex] `spring-cloud-platform` artifact. Local builds resolve that platform, `service-web`, and
[codex] `service-core` from `mavenLocal()` first; after changing service-common platform metadata, publish it
[codex] locally before rebuilding this service.
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew clean build       # Clean and build
[codex] ./gradlew test              # Run tests
[codex] ./gradlew test jacocoTestReport  # Generate JaCoCo coverage reports
[codex] ./gradlew spotlessCheck     # Check code style
[codex] ./gradlew clean spotlessApply  # Apply code formatting
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] JaCoCo writes the HTML report to `build/reports/jacoco/test/html/index.html`
[codex] and the XML report to `build/reports/jacoco/test/jacocoTestReport.xml`.
[codex] `check` enforces the Phase 2 coverage gates: 90% line coverage and 85% branch
[codex] coverage. The recorded baseline is 96.43% line / 88.71% branch; ratchet toward
[codex] critical utility and provider path coverage.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Testing
[codex] 
[codex] Before writing or changing tests, consult the
[codex] [canonical service-common testing patterns](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/service-common/blob/main/docs/testing-patterns.md).
[codex] Keep application-owned Spring beans real. Integration tests use Testcontainers for PostgreSQL,
[codex] Redis, and RabbitMQ, and use WireMock only for the external FRED HTTP boundary.
[codex] 
[codex] Docker must be running, but the integration suite does not require shared local infrastructure or a
[codex] real FRED API key. `AbstractIntegrationTest` supplies the containers, and tests that call FRED extend
[codex] `AbstractWireMockTest` and register controlled FRED responses through `FredApiStubs`.
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Run one integration suite
[codex] ./gradlew test --tests '*EventListenerIntegrationTest'
[codex] 
[codex] # Run all messaging integration suites
[codex] ./gradlew test --tests '*EventListenerIntegrationTest' \
[codex]   --tests '*MessageConsumerIntegrationTest' \
[codex]   --tests '*EndToEndMessagingFlowIntegrationTest'
[codex] ```
[codex]

[codex] # Dependency Automation
[codex] 
[codex] The workspace-wide operating policy, activation steps, cost boundary, and
[codex] failure triage are owned by
[codex] [orchestration's dependency automation guide](../../orchestration/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] This document records only the `service-common` integration and review checks.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Update discovery
[codex] 
[codex] `renovate.json` extends the shared Budget Analyzer preset. Renovate's native
[codex] Gradle, Gradle Wrapper, and GitHub Actions managers discover this repository's
[codex] version catalog, build scripts, wrapper distribution, and workflow actions.
[codex] There are currently no Dockerfiles in this repository. Add file-specific
[codex] manager configuration only if a future dependency declaration is not covered
[codex] by a native manager.
[codex] 
[codex] The Phase 3 local extraction found 52 dependency occurrences across 12 files:
[codex] 33 Gradle occurrences in seven files, one wrapper occurrence, and 18 GitHub
[codex] Actions occurrences in four workflows. Maven and Gradle lookups produced 41
[codex] candidate branches before scheduling, concurrency limits, and dashboard
[codex] approval. Maintained-line and later-major proposals were both visible for the
[codex] Spring platform, documentation, test, locking, and wrapper dependencies. The
[codex] Actions references were extracted, but authenticated GitHub lookups remain a
[codex] post-publication hosted check because no GitHub token is supplied to local
[codex] validation.
[codex] 
[codex] Renovate proposes changes only to direct declarations. It does not turn every
[codex] version inherited from the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, or Spring Modulith BOMs
[codex] into an independently updateable declaration. The resolved dependency graph
[codex] described below is the security inventory for those inherited versions.
[codex] 
[codex] Major updates remain visible in the Dependency Dashboard and require approval.
[codex] In particular, Spring Boot 4 and the related Spring Cloud, Spring Framework,
[codex] Spring Security, Spring Modulith, SpringDoc, Testcontainers, ShedLock, and test
[codex] platform lines are a compatibility migration, not a routine patch. Renovate
[codex] cannot select the correct release-train combination or coordinate consumer
[codex] services by itself.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Resolved dependency graph
[codex] 
[codex] `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml` runs on trusted pushes to `main`,
[codex] weekly, and by manual dispatch. The job runs only for the `main` ref and uses
[codex] the official `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action with the open-source
[codex] `basic` cache provider. Its job token receives only `contents: write`, which is
[codex] required by GitHub's Dependency Submission API. The graph is submitted
[codex] directly and is not retained as a workflow artifact or published as a Build
[codex] Scan.
[codex] 
[codex] The action's default resolution task visits all projects and all resolvable
[codex] configurations. Do not add project or configuration filters without proving
[codex] equivalent coverage: the platform modules contribute imported BOMs, while the
[codex] library modules contribute compile, runtime, and test dependency trees.
[codex] 
[codex] Local Phase 3 validation generated an official plugin snapshot without
[codex] submitting it. The snapshot contained 216 resolved coordinate/version entries
[codex] across the four subprojects. It included the Spring Boot, Spring Cloud, and
[codex] Spring Modulith BOM coordinates and these inherited component families where
[codex] the current build actually resolves them:
[codex] 
[codex] | Component family | Resolution evidence |
[codex] | --- | --- |
[codex] | Spring Framework | Library runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] | Spring Security | `service-core` and `service-web` test classpaths; compile-only APIs are also resolved through compile classpaths |
[codex] | Embedded Tomcat | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the servlet stack |
[codex] | Netty | `service-web` test runtime, which exercises the reactive stack |
[codex] | Jackson | `service-core` and `service-web` runtime and test classpaths |
[codex] 
[codex] The entry count and resolved versions will change as the checked-in dependency
[codex] selection changes; they are evidence from onboarding, not a desired-version
[codex] inventory. A successful local snapshot proves generation and transitive
[codex] coverage, but only a hosted run after repository activation proves submission
[codex] and creates Dependabot alert input.
[codex] 
[codex] GitHub's dependency graph reports resolved packages but does not decide which
[codex] BOM or override should remediate an inherited finding. A Dependabot alert is
[codex] also not proof that an available Spring Boot update fixes every Framework,
[codex] Security, Jackson, Tomcat, or Netty advisory. Trace the affected configuration
[codex] with Gradle dependency insight and review any override as a service-owner
[codex] decision.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Bot pull request checks
[codex] 
[codex] For every Renovate pull request:
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Keep the shared preset's no-automerge and dashboard-approval behavior.
[codex] 2. Review the resolved dependency diff, release notes, Java 25 and Spring
[codex]    release-train compatibility, and whether an inherited advisory is actually
[codex]    remediated.
[codex] 3. Run the repository-required validation in order:
[codex] 
[codex]    ```bash
[codex]    ./gradlew clean spotlessApply
[codex]    ./gradlew clean build
[codex]    ```
[codex] 
[codex] 4. For platform or shared-library changes, validate all consuming services
[codex]    before release as required by
[codex]    [versioning and compatibility](versioning-and-compatibility.md). Do not
[codex]    publish artifacts or update consumers from an unreviewed bot branch.
[codex] 
[codex] Graph generation failures are failures, not clean security results. Preserve
[codex] dependency-resolution and submission errors for triage; do not substitute
[codex] Maven Local, omit configurations, or enable a hosted Build Scan to make the job
[codex] pass.

[codex] [codex] # The `dependency-submission` action
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The `gradle/actions/dependency-submission` action provides the simplest (and recommended) way to generate a 
[codex] [codex] dependency graph for your project. This action will attempt to detect all dependencies used by your build
[codex] [codex] without building and testing the project itself.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The dependency graph snapshot is generated via integration with the [GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin), and submitted to your repository via the 
[codex] [codex] [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] [codex] The generated snapshot files can be submitted in the same job, or saved for submission in a subsequent job.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The generated dependency graph includes all of the dependencies in your build, and is used by GitHub to generate 
[codex] [codex] [Dependabot Alerts](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/dependabot-alerts/about-dependabot-alerts) 
[codex] [codex] for vulnerable dependencies, as well as to populate the 
[codex] [codex] [Dependency Graph insights view](https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/supply-chain-security/understanding-your-software-supply-chain/exploring-the-dependencies-of-a-repository#viewing-the-dependency-graph).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If you're confused by the behaviour you're seeing or have specific questions, please check out [the FAQ](dependency-submission-faq.md) before raising an issue.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] [codex] > ### ⚡️ Choice of caching providers in v6
[codex] [codex] > To provide the fastest possible build experience this action includes **Enhanced Caching** via `gradle-actions-caching`, an optimized provider powered by proprietary technology. This feature is **free for all public repositories** and is currently available as a **Free Preview** for private repositories. 
[codex] [codex] >
[codex] [codex] > **Prefer a 100% Open Source (MIT) path?**
[codex] [codex] > We also provide a **Basic Caching** provider as a thin wrapper over `actions/cache`. This provider is **free for all repositories** (public and private) and can be enabled at any time by setting `cache-provider: basic`.
[codex] [codex] >
[codex] [codex] > For a full breakdown of the components, usage tiers, and our **Safe Harbor** data privacy commitment, see our [Distribution & Licensing Guide](../DISTRIBUTION.md).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## General usage
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The following workflow will generate a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submit it immediately to the repository via the
[codex] [codex] Dependency Submission API. For most projects, this default configuration should be all that you need.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Simply add this as a new workflow file to your repository (eg `.github/workflows/dependency-submission.yml`).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] on:
[codex] [codex]   push:
[codex] [codex]     branches: [ 'main' ]
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] permissions:
[codex] [codex]   contents: write
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] jobs:
[codex] [codex]   dependency-submission:
[codex] [codex]     runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] [codex]     steps:
[codex] [codex]     - uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] [codex]     - uses: actions/setup-java@v5
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         distribution: temurin
[codex] [codex]         java-version: 17
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Gradle execution
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To generate a dependency graph, the `dependency-submission` action must perform a Gradle execution that resolves
[codex] [codex] the dependencies of the project. All dependencies that are resolved in this execution will be included in the 
[codex] [codex] generated dependency graph.  By default action executes a built-in task that is designed to resolve all build dependencies
[codex] [codex] (`:ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies`).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The action looks for a Gradle project in the root of the workspace, and executes this project with
[codex] [codex] the Gradle wrapper, if configured for the project. If the wrapper is not configured, whatever `gradle` available
[codex] [codex] on the command-line will be used.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The action provides the ability to override the Gradle version and task to execute, as well as provide 
[codex] [codex] additional arguments that will be passed to Gradle on the command-line. See [Configuration Parameters](#configuration-parameters) below.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Selecting a cache provider
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] See [Selecting a cache provider](setup-gradle.md#selecting-a-cache-provider) for details on choosing between the `enhanced` (default) and `basic` cache providers via the `cache-provider` input.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Disabling caching
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Caching is enabled by default. You can disable caching for the action as follows:
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex] cache-disabled: true
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Publishing a Develocity Build Scan® from your dependency submission workflow
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] You can automatically publish a free Develocity Build Scan on every run of `gradle/actions/dependency-submission`. 
[codex] [codex] Three input parameters are required, one to enable publishing and two more to accept the 
[codex] [codex] [Develocity terms of use](https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         build-scan-publish: true
[codex] [codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-url: "https://gradle.com/help/legal-terms-of-use"
[codex] [codex]         build-scan-terms-of-use-agree: "yes"
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] A Build Scan makes it easy to determine the source of any dependency vulnerabilities in your project.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Configuration parameters
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In some cases, the default action configuration will not be sufficient, and additional action parameters will need to be specified.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate and save dependency graph
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         # Use a particular Gradle version instead of the configured wrapper.
[codex] [codex]         gradle-version: '8.6'
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # The gradle project is not in the root of the repository.
[codex] [codex]         build-root-directory: my-gradle-project
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Choose a task that will trigger dependency resolution
[codex] [codex]         dependency-resolution-task: myDependencyResolutionTask
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Additional arguments that should be passed to execute Gradle
[codex] [codex]         additional-arguments: --no-configuration-cache
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Enable configuration-cache reuse for this build.
[codex] [codex]         cache-encryption-key: ${{ secrets.GRADLE_ENCRYPTION_KEY }}
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Do not attempt to submit the dependency-graph. Save it as a workflow artifact.
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph: generate-and-upload
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Change the number of days that workflow artifacts are retained. (Default is 30 days).
[codex] [codex]         artifact-retention-days: 5
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # Specify the location where dependency graph files will be generated.
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph-report-dir: custom-report-dir
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]         # By default, failure to generate a dependency graph will cause the workflow to fail
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph-continue-on-failure: true
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] See the [Action Metadata file](../dependency-submission/action.yml) for a more detailed description of each input parameter.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin` can be further 
[codex] [codex] [configured via a number of environment variables](https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin?#required-environment-variables). 
[codex] [codex] These will be automatically set by the `dependency-submission` action, but you may override these values 
[codex] [codex] by setting them explicitly in your workflow file.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Reducing storage costs for saved dependency graph artifacts
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] By default, the dependency graph that is generated is stored as a workflow artifact.
[codex] [codex] To reduce storage costs for these artifacts, you can:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] 1. Set the `artifact-retention-days`:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate dependency graph but only store workflow artifacts for 1 day
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         artifact-retention-days: 1 # Default is 30 days or as configured for repository
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] 2. Disable storing dependency-graph artifacts using `generate-and-submit`
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph but do not store as workflow artifact
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph: 'generate-and-submit' # Default value is 'generate-submit-and-upload'
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Resolving a dependency vulnerability
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Finding the source of a dependency vulnerability
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Once you have submitted a dependency graph, you may receive Dependabot Alerts warning about vulnerabilities in
[codex] [codex] dependencies of your project. In the case of transitive dependencies, it may not be obvious how that dependency is
[codex] [codex] used or what you can do to address the vulnerability alert.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The first step to investigating a Dependabot Alert is to determine the source of the dependency. One of the best ways to 
[codex] [codex] do so is with a free Develocity Build Scan®, which makes it easy to explore the dependencies resolved in your build.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] &lt;img width="1069" alt="image" src="https://github.com/gradle/actions/assets/179734/3a637dfd-396c-4e94-8332-dcc6eb5a35ac">
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In this example, we are searching for dependencies matching the name 'com.squareup.okio:okio' in the _Build Dependencies_ of 
[codex] [codex] the project. You can easily see that this dependency originates from 'com.github.ben-manes:gradle-versions-plugin'.
[codex] [codex] Knowing the source of the dependency can help determine how to deal with the Dependabot Alert.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Note that you may need to look at both the _Dependencies_ and the _Build Dependencies_ of your project to find the
[codex] [codex] offending dependency.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### When you cannot publish a Build Scan®
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If publishing a free Build Scan to https://scans.gradle.com isn't an option, and you don't have access to a private [Develocity
[codex] [codex] server](https://gradle.com/) for your project, you can obtain information about the each resolved dependency by running the `dependency-submission` workflow with debug logging enabled.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The simplest way to do so is to re-run the dependency-submission job with debug logging enabled:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] &lt;img width="665" alt="image" src="https://github.com/gradle/actions/assets/179734/d95b889a-09fb-4731-91f2-baebbf647e31">
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] When you do so, the Gradle build that generates the dependency-graph will include a log message for each dependency version included in the graph.
[codex] [codex] Given the details in one log message, you can run (locally) the built-in [dependencyInsight](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/viewing_debugging_dependencies.html#dependency_insights) task
[codex] [codex] to determine exactly how the dependency was resolved. 
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For example, given the following message in the logs:
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] Detected dependency 'com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre': project = ':my-subproject', configuration = 'compileClasspath'
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] You would run the following command locally:
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:dependencyInsight --configuration compileClasspath --dependency com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### Dealing with 'classpath' configuration
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If the configuration value in the log message is "classpath" then instead of running `dependency-insight` you'll need to run the Gradle
[codex] [codex] `buildEnvironment` task.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For example, given the following message in the logs:
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] Detected dependency 'xerces:xercesImpl:2.12.2': project = ':my-subproject', configuration = 'classpath'
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] You would run the following command locally to expose the `xercesImpl` dependency:
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:buildEnvironment | grep -C 5 xercesImpl
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Updating the dependency version
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Once you've discovered the source of the dependency, the most obvious fix is to update the dependency to a patched version that does not
[codex] [codex] suffer the vulnerability. For direct dependencies, this is often straightforward.  But for transitive dependencies it can be tricky.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Dependency source is specified directly in the build
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If the dependency is used to compile your code or run your tests, it's normal for the underlying "source" of the dependency to have a
[codex] [codex] version configured directly in the build. For example, if you have a vulnerable version of `com.squareup.okio:okio` in your `compileClasspath`, then
[codex] [codex] it's likely you have a dependency like `com.squareup.moshi:moshi` configured as an `api` or `implementation` dependency.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In this case there are 2 possibilities:
[codex] [codex] 1. There is a newer, compatible version of `com.squareup.moshi:moshi` available, and you can just bump the version number.
[codex] [codex] 2. There isn't a newer, compatible version of `com.squareup.moshi:moshi`
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In the second case, you can add a Dependency Constraint, to force the use of the newest version of `com.squareup.okio`:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] dependencies {
[codex] [codex]   implementation("com.squareup.moshi:moshi:1.12.0")
[codex] [codex]   constraints {
[codex] [codex]     // Force a newer version of okio in transitive resolution
[codex] [codex]     implementation("com.squareup.okio:okio:3.6.0")
[codex] [codex]   }
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Dependency source is a plugin classpath
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If the vulnerable dependency is introduced by a Gradle plugin, again the best option is to look for a newer version of the plugin.
[codex] [codex] But if none is available, you can still use a dependency constraint to force a newer transitive version to be used.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The dependency constraint must be added to the `classpath` configuration of the buildscript that loads the plugin.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```kotlin
[codex] [codex] buildscript {
[codex] [codex]   repositories {
[codex] [codex]     gradlePluginPortal()
[codex] [codex]   }
[codex] [codex]   dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     constraints {
[codex] [codex]       // Force a newer version of okio in transitive resolution
[codex] [codex]       classpath("com.squareup.okio:okio:3.6.0")
[codex] [codex]     }
[codex] [codex]   }
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] plugins {
[codex] [codex]   id("com.github.ben-manes.versions") version("0.51.0")
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Limiting the dependencies that appear in the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] By default, the `dependency-submission` action attempts to detect all dependencies declared and used by your Gradle build.
[codex] [codex] At times it may helpful to limit the dependencies reported to GitHub, to avoid security alerts for dependencies that 
[codex] [codex] don't form a critical part of your product. For example, a vulnerability in the tool you use to generate documentation 
[codex] [codex] may not be as important as a vulnerability in one of your runtime dependencies.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The `dependency-submission` action provides a convenient mechanism to filter the projects and configurations that
[codex] [codex] contribute to the dependency graph.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] > [!NOTE]
[codex] [codex] > Ideally, all dependencies involved in building and testing a project will be extracted and reported in a dependency graph. 
[codex] [codex] > These dependencies would be assigned to different scopes (eg development, runtime, testing) and the GitHub UI would make it easy to opt-in to security alerts for different dependency scopes.
[codex] [codex] > However, this functionality does not yet exist.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Selecting Gradle projects that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If you do not want the dependency graph to include dependencies from every project in your build, 
[codex] [codex] you can easily exclude or include certain projects from the dependency extraction process.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To restrict which Gradle subprojects contribute to the report, specify which projects to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] [codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-projects` and `dependency-graph-include-projects` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Note that excluding a project in this way only removes dependencies that are _resolved_ as part of that project, and may
[codex] [codex] not necessarily remove all dependencies _declared_ in that project. If another project depends on the excluded project
[codex] [codex] then it may transitively resolve dependencies declared in the excluded project: these dependencies will still be included
[codex] [codex] in the generated dependency graph.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Selecting Gradle configurations that will contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Similarly to Gradle projects, it is possible to exclude or include a set of dependency configurations from dependency graph generation,
[codex] [codex] so that only dependencies resolved by the included configurations are reported.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To restrict which Gradle configurations contribute to the report, specify which configurations to exclude or include via a regular expression.
[codex] [codex] You can use the `dependency-graph-exclude-configurations` and `dependency-graph-include-configurations` input parameters for this purpose.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Note that configuration exclusion applies to the configuration in which the dependency is _resolved_ which is not necessarily
[codex] [codex] the configuration where the dependency is _declared_. For example if you decare a dependency as `implementation` in
[codex] [codex] a Java project, that dependency will be resolved in `compileClasspath`, `runtimeClasspath` and possibly other configurations.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Example of project and configuration filtering
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For example, if you want to exclude dependencies resolved by the `buildSrc` project, and exclude dependencies from the `testCompileClasspath` and `testRuntimeClasspath` configurations, you would use the following configuration:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex]     - name: Generate and submit dependency graph
[codex] [codex]       uses: gradle/actions/dependency-submission@v6
[codex] [codex]       with:
[codex] [codex]         # Exclude all dependencies that originate solely in the 'buildSrc' project
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-projects: ':buildSrc'
[codex] [codex]         # Exclude dependencies that are only resolved in test classpaths
[codex] [codex]         dependency-graph-exclude-configurations: '.*[Tt]est(Compile|Runtime)Classpath'
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Advance usage scenarios
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Using a custom plugin repository
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] By default, the action downloads the `github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin` from the Gradle Plugin Portal (https://plugins.gradle.org). If your GitHub Actions environment does not have access to this URL, you can specify a custom plugin repository to use with an environment variable.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] See [the setup-gradle docs](setup-gradle.md#using-a-custom-plugin-repository) for details.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Integrating the `dependency-review-action`
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The GitHub [dependency-review-action](https://github.com/actions/dependency-review-action) helps you 
[codex] [codex] understand dependency changes (and the security impact of these changes) for a pull request,
[codex] [codex] by comparing the dependency graph for the pull-request with that of the HEAD commit.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Integrating the Dependency Review Action requires 2 changes to your workflows:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### 1. Add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing Dependency Submission workflow.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] In order to perform Dependency Review on a pull request, the dependency graph must be submitted for the pull request.
[codex] [codex] To do this, simply add a `pull_request` trigger to your existing dependency submission workflow.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```yaml
[codex] [codex] name: Dependency Submission
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] on:
[codex] [codex]   push:
[codex] [codex]     branches: [ 'main' ]
[codex] [codex]   pull_request:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] permissions:
[codex] [codex]   contents: write
[codex] [codex] name: Gradle Dependency Submission
[codex] [codex] description: Generates a dependency graph for a Gradle project and submits it via the Dependency Submission API
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] inputs:
[codex] [codex]   # Gradle execution configuration
[codex] [codex]   gradle-version:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       Gradle version to use. If specified, this Gradle version will be downloaded, added to the PATH and used for invoking Gradle.
[codex] [codex]       If not provided, it is assumed that the project uses the Gradle Wrapper.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   build-root-directory:
[codex] [codex]     description: Path to the root directory of the build. Default is the root of the GitHub workspace.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   dependency-resolution-task:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       Task(s) that should be executed in order to resolve all project dependencies.
[codex] [codex]       By default, the built-in `:ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies` task is executed.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   additional-arguments:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       Additional arguments to pass to Gradle when generating the dependency graph.
[codex] [codex]       For example, `--no-configuration-cache --stacktrace`.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   # Cache configuration
[codex] [codex]   cache-provider:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       Specifies the caching implementation to use.
[codex] [codex]       'enhanced' (default) uses the full-featured commercial caching service (gradle-actions-caching).
[codex] [codex]       'basic' uses a simple open-source caching implementation based on GitHub Actions cache.
[codex] [codex]       'external' disables Gradle User Home caching by this action, for use when Gradle User Home is already saved and restored by another mechanism (e.g. Develocity Artifact Cache).
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]     default: 'enhanced'
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   cache-disabled:
[codex] [codex]     description: When 'true', all caching is disabled. No entries will be written to or read from the cache.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]     default: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   cache-read-only:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       When 'true', existing entries will be read from the cache but no entries will be written.
[codex] [codex]       By default this value is 'false' for workflows on the GitHub default branch and 'true' for workflows on other branches.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]     default: ${{ github.event.repository != null && github.ref_name != github.event.repository.default_branch }}
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   cache-write-only:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       When 'true', entries will not be restored from the cache but will be saved at the end of the Job.
[codex] [codex]       Setting this to 'true' implies cache-read-only will be 'false'.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]     default: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   cache-overwrite-existing:
[codex] [codex]     description: When 'true', a pre-existing Gradle User Home will not prevent the cache from being restored.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]     default: false
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   cache-encryption-key:
[codex] [codex]     description: |
[codex] [codex]       A base64 encoded AES key used to encrypt the configuration-cache data. The key is exported as 'GRADLE_ENCRYPTION_KEY' for later steps.
[codex] [codex]       A suitable key can be generated with `openssl rand -base64 16`.
[codex] [codex]       Configuration-cache data will not be saved/restored without an encryption key being provided.
[codex] [codex]     required: false
[codex] [codex]

[codex] [codex]       By default ('on-success'), cleanup is performed when all Gradle builds succeed for the Job. 
[codex] [codex]       This behaviour can be disabled ('never'), or configured to always run irrespective of the build outcome ('always').
[codex] [codex]       Valid values are 'never', 'on-success' and 'always'.

[codex] [codex]       By default a dependency-graph will be generated, submitted to the dependency-submission API, and saved as a workflow artifact.

[codex] [codex] # GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] A Gradle plugin for generating a GitHub dependency graph for a Gradle build, which can be uploaded to the [GitHub Dependency Submission API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission).
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Usage
[codex] [codex] This plugin is designed to be used in a GitHub Actions workflow, and is tightly integrated into the 
[codex] [codex] [gradle/actions/dependency-submission](https://github.com/gradle/actions/tree/main/dependency-submission) action.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For other uses, the [core plugin](https://plugins.gradle.org/plugin/org.gradle.github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin) (`org.gradle.github.GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin`) 
[codex] [codex] should be applied to the `Gradle` instance via a Gradle init script as follows:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```groovy
[codex] [codex] import org.gradle.github.GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] [codex] initscript {
[codex] [codex]   repositories {
[codex] [codex]     maven {
[codex] [codex]       url = uri("https://plugins.gradle.org/m2/")
[codex] [codex]     }
[codex] [codex]   }
[codex] [codex]   dependencies {
[codex] [codex]     classpath("org.gradle:github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin:+")
[codex] [codex]   }
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] apply plugin: GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] This causes 2 separate plugins to be applied, that can be used independently:
[codex] [codex] - `GitHubDependencyExtractorPlugin` collects all dependencies that are resolved during a build execution and writes these to a file. The output file can be found at `&lt;root>/build/reports/github-depenency-graph-snapshots/&lt;job-correlator>.json`.
[codex] [codex] - `ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin` creates a `ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies` task that will attempt to resolve all dependencies for a Gradle build, by simply invoking `dependencies` on all projects.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Environment variables
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The following environment variables configure the snapshot generated by the `GitHubDependencyExtractorPlugin`. See the [GitHub Dependency Submission API docs](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/dependency-graph/dependency-submission?apiVersion=2022-11-28) for details:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### Required environment variables
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_CORRELATOR`: Sets the `job.correlator` value for the dependency submission
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_JOB_ID`: Sets the `job.id` value for the dependency submission
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REF`: Sets the `ref` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_SHA`: Sets the `sha` value for the commit that generated the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_WORKSPACE`: Sets the root directory of the github repository. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] [codex] - `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` (optional): Specifies where the dependency graph report will be generated. Must be an absolute path.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### Optional environment variables
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_NAME`: Sets the `detector.name` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `GitHub Dependency Graph Gradle Plugin`
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_VERSION`: Sets the `detector.version` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to current version of the plugin.
[codex] [codex] - `GITHUB_DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_DETECTOR_URL`: Sets the `detector.url` value for the dependency submission. Defaults to `https://github.com/gradle/github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin`
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Each of these values can also be provided via a system property. 
[codex] [codex] eg: Env var `DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR` can be set with `-DDEPENDENCY_GRAPH_REPORT_DIR=...` on the command-line.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Filtering which Gradle Configurations contribute to the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If you do not want to include every dependency configuration in every project in your build, you can limit the
[codex] [codex] dependency extraction to a subset of these.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The following parameters control the set of projects and configurations that contribute dependencies.
[codex] [codex] Each of these is a regular expression value, and can set either as an environment variable or as a system property on the command line.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] | Property                                | Description               | Default                         |
[codex] [codex] |-----------------------------------------|---------------------------|---------------------------------|
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_INCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects to include       | All projects are included       |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_EXCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects to exclude       | No projects are excluded        |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_INCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations to include | All configurations are included |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_EXCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations to exclude | No configurations are excluded  |
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Controlling the scope of dependencies in the dependency graph
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The GitHub dependency graph allows a scope to be assigned to each reported dependency.
[codex] [codex] The only permissible values for scope are 'runtime' and 'development'.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The following parameters control the set of projects and configurations that provide 'runtime' scoped dependencies.
[codex] [codex] Any dependency resolution that does not match these parameters will be scoped 'development'.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Each of these parameters is a regular expression value, and can set either as an environment variable or as a system property on the command line.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] | Property                                        | Description                                               | Default                         |
[codex] [codex] |-------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------|
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_INCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects that can provide 'runtime' dependencies          | All projects are included       |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_EXCLUDE_PROJECTS       | Projects that do not provide 'runtime' dependencies       | No projects are excluded        |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_INCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations that contain 'runtime' dependencies        | All configurations are included |
[codex] [codex] | DEPENDENCY_GRAPH_RUNTIME_EXCLUDE_CONFIGURATIONS | Configurations that do not contain 'runtime' dependencies | No configurations are excluded  |
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] By default, no scope is assigned to dependencies in the graph. To enable scopes in the generated dependency graph,
[codex] [codex] at least one of these parameters must be configured.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For dependencies that are resolved in multiple projects and/or multiple configurations, only a single 'runtime' scoped resolution
[codex] [codex] is required for that dependency to be scoped 'runtime'.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Gradle compatibility
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The plugin is compatible with most versions of Gradle >= 5.2, and all changes are tested against a range of versions.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The plugin is compatible with running Gradle with the configuration-cache enabled: this support is
[codex] [codex] limited to Gradle "8.1.0" and later. Earlier Gradle versions will not work with `--configuration-cache`.
[codex] [codex] Note that no dependency graph will be generated when configuration state is loaded from the configuration-cache.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] | Gradle version | Compatible | Compatible with configuration-cache |

[codex] [codex] | 7.1 - 7.6.6 | ✅ | :x: |
[codex] [codex] | 8.0 - 8.0.2 | ✅ | :x: |

[codex] [codex] | 9.0.0 - 9.4.1 | ✅ | ✅ |
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Dependency verification
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] When using this plugin with [dependency signature verification enabled](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/dependency_verification.html#sec:signature-verification), 
[codex] [codex] the you should be able to update your `dependency-verification.xml` file using `--write-verification-metadata pgp,sha256`.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] However, if this doesn't work, you can add the following to your `dependency-verificaton.xml` file:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```xml
[codex] [codex] &lt;trusted-keys>
[codex] [codex]    &lt;trusted-key id="7B79ADD11F8A779FE90FD3D0893A028475557671" group="org.gradle" name="github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin"/>
[codex] [codex] &lt;/trusted-keys>
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Using the plugin to generate dependency reports
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] As well as the `GitHubDependencyGraphPlugin`, which is tailored for use by the [gradle/actions/dependency-submission](https://github.com/gradle/actions/tree/main/dependency-submission) GitHub Action, this repository also provides the `SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin`, which generates dependency-graph outputs in simple text format.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To use the `SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin` you'll need to create an `init.gradle` file to apply the plugin to your project:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```groovy
[codex] [codex] initscript {
[codex] [codex]     repositories {
[codex] [codex]         gradlePluginPortal()
[codex] [codex]     }
[codex] [codex]     dependencies {
[codex] [codex]         classpath "org.gradle:github-dependency-graph-gradle-plugin:+"
[codex] [codex]     }
[codex] [codex] }
[codex] [codex] apply plugin: org.gradle.dependencygraph.simple.SimpleDependencyGraphPlugin
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] and then execute the task to resolve all dependencies in your project:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```shell
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew -I init.gradle --dependency-verification=off --no-configuration-cache --no-configure-on-demand :ForceDependencyResolutionPlugin_resolveAllDependencies
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] You'll find the generated files in `build/reports/dependency-graph-snapshots`.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Using dependency reports to determine the underlying source of a dependency
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] After generating the dependency reports as described, it is possible to determine the dependency source by:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] 1. Locate the dependency (including matching version) in the `dependency-resolution.json` file.
[codex] [codex] 2. Inspect each `resolvedBy` entry for the `path` and `configuration` values. The `scope` value is unimportant in this context.
[codex] [codex] 3. Use the built-in [dependencyInsight](https://docs.gradle.org/current/userguide/viewing_debugging_dependencies.html#dependency_insights) task to determine exactly how the dependency was resolved. The `path` indicates the project where the task should be executed, and the `configuration` is an input to the task.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] For example, given the following from the `dependency-resolution.json` report:
[codex] [codex] ```json
[codex] [codex]   "dependency" : "com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre",
[codex] [codex]   "effectiveScope" : "Unknown",
[codex] [codex]   "resolvedBy" : [ {
[codex] [codex]     "path" : ":my-subproject",
[codex] [codex]     "configuration" : "compileClasspath",
[codex] [codex]     "scope" : "Unknown"
[codex] [codex]   }, ...
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] You would run the command:
[codex] [codex] ```shell
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew :my-subproject:dependencyInsight --configuration compileClasspath --dependency com.google.guava:guava:32.1.3-jre
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] #### Dealing with 'classpath' configuration
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] If the configuration value in `dependency-resolution.json` is "classpath", or for some other reason the above instructions do not work,
[codex] [codex] it is possible to recostruct the full resolution path using the generated `dependency-graph.json` file.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Search for the exact dependency version in `dependency-graph.json`, and you'll see an "id" entry for that dependency as well as one or more
[codex] [codex] "dependencies" entries. By tracing back through the dependencies you can determine the underlying source of the dependency.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Building/Testing
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To build and test this plugin, run the following task:
[codex] [codex] ```shell
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew check
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] To self-test this plugin and generate a dependency graph for this repository, run:
[codex] [codex] ```shell
[codex] [codex] ./plugin-self-test-local
[codex] [codex] ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] The generated dependency graph will be submitted to GitHub only if you supply a
[codex] [codex] [GitHub API token](https://docs.github.com/en/authentication/keeping-your-account-and-data-secure/creating-a-personal-access-token)
[codex] [codex] via the environment variable `GITHUB_TOKEN`.

[codex] [codex] # Currency Service Agent Instructions
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Tree Position
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] **Archetype:** service  
[codex] [codex] **Scope:** Budget Analyzer ecosystem  
[codex] [codex] **Role:** Manages currencies and exchange rates with external provider
[codex] [codex] integration.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ### Relationships and Boundaries
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - Consume shared Spring patterns from `../service-common/`.
[codex] [codex] - Treat `../orchestration/` as the owner of ecosystem coordination and runtime
[codex] [codex]   infrastructure.
[codex] [codex] - Discover peer services instead of maintaining a static list:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex]   ```bash
[codex] [codex]   find .. -mindepth 1 -maxdepth 1 -type d -name '*-service' -print | sort
[codex] [codex]   ```
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - Read this repository, `../service-common/`, and `../orchestration/docs/` as
[codex] [codex]   needed. Read `../ai-session-handler/docs/plan-format.md` only when producing
[codex] [codex]   an AI Session Handler plan.
[codex] [codex] - Write only within this repository. Do not modify sibling repositories.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Operating Rules
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] - Do not use agent or subagent tools for code exploration. Inspect the workspace
[codex] [codex]   directly with `rg`, `find`, and file reads.
[codex] [codex] - Never run Git write commands, including `commit`, `push`, `checkout`, `reset`,
[codex] [codex]   or branch manipulation, unless the user explicitly requests them. The user
[codex] [codex]   controls Git operations.
[codex] [codex] - Keep implementations simple. Choose the most direct design that handles
[codex] [codex]   realistic inputs, states, and failure modes without weakening security, data
[codex] [codex]   integrity, or required behavior.
[codex] [codex] - Put request shape and syntax validation at the API boundary. Put business
[codex] [codex]   invariants, ownership, persistence state, and cross-entity rules in services.
[codex] [codex] - Do not duplicate validation across layers unless another caller can bypass the
[codex] [codex]   validated boundary or the service owns the rule.
[codex] [codex] - Add defensive branches only for states that can plausibly arise and have a
[codex] [codex]   useful response. Handle failures at external and asynchronous boundaries
[codex] [codex]   explicitly.
[codex] [codex] - Never commit credentials or replace missing infrastructure, credentials, or
[codex] [codex]   shared artifacts with hard-coded workarounds.
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ## Discovery
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] Use discovery commands instead of relying on copied inventories:
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] ```bash
[codex] [codex] # Repository structure and build entry points
[codex] [codex] find . -maxdepth 2 -type f -not -path './.git/*' | sort
[codex] [codex] ./gradlew tasks
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Java packages, controllers, and routes
[codex] [codex] find src/main/java/org/budgetanalyzer/currency -type d | sort
[codex] [codex] rg -n '@(Request|Get|Post|Put|Patch|Delete)Mapping' src/main/java
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Providers, domain events, and distributed patterns
[codex] [codex] find src/main/java -type f -name '*Provider.java' -o -path '*/domain/event/*.java'
[codex] [codex] rg -n '@Scheduled|@SchedulerLock|@Cacheable|@CacheEvict|@ApplicationModuleListener' src/main/java
[codex] [codex] 
[codex] [codex] # Runtime configuration and environment variables
[codex] [codex] rg -n '^currency-service:|fred:|shedlock|cache|rabbit|redis' src/main/resources/application.yml
[codex] [codex] rg -n '\$\{[^}]+}' src/main/resources/application.yml
[codex] [codex] ```
